I just sit in my room all day in the same clothes. I used to do things—like knit, or write in my journal, or go for walks—but I just don't do any of that now. And when my mom asks me what's wrong, I just... I barely have the energy to even answer her. So she brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before in the past, perhaps months or years ago, or is this the first time you have ever felt this low?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I think this is the first time. I don't remember ever feeling this... empty before. I always used to do things, so... yeah, this is the first time it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I haven't heard anything like that. Or seen anything either. It's really just... quiet. I haven't noticed any voices or anything weird.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your physical movements, like feeling as though you are moving in slow motion or, conversely, feeling so restless that you cannot sit still?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I think I'm eating about the same amount as usual. I haven't noticed any changes in my weight, either. I just... I eat whatever is there, I guess. Nothing has really changed with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns, such as having trouble falling asleep or waking up much earlier than you intended?\n\nPatient: Um... no, my sleep has been pretty normal. I usually just go to bed and sleep through the night. I haven't really had any trouble falling asleep, and I wake up around the same time I always do. So... no changes there.\n\nDoctor: How much difficulty are you having with your daily responsibilities, such as schoolwork or taking care of your basic hygiene, compared to how you functioned before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm having a lot of difficulty, actually. I had to stop moving forward in my certificate program. I just... I'm not able to handle the practicum right now. And I can't really plan for the clinic office job I was working toward, either. Everything just kind of stopped. I just can't do it right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling worthless, or do you often blame yourself for things that have gone wrong in your life lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't feel that way.\n\nDoctor: About how long has it been since you started feeling this way and stopped doing your usual activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... this whole decline, all of this happening... it's been going on since I started the community-college program. I barely have the energy to answer her.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have any desire or interest in spending time with other people, or do you find yourself preferring to be completely alone?\n\nPatient: I prefer to be completely alone. It's like I just don't have the energy to start. I just sit in my room with the same clothes on. I don't knit or write in my journal or go walking like I used to. Even when my mom asks what's wrong, I... I barely have the energy to answer her.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have any desire or interest in spending time with other people, or do you find yourself preferring to be completely alone?\n\nPatient: I prefer to be completely alone. So, no, I don't have any interest in seeing anyone else.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting in your room alone, do you find that your thoughts tend to get jumbled or disorganized, making it difficult to follow a train of thought?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. My thoughts don't really get jumbled up like that. I mean, when I'm just sitting there, I'm not really... I don't have a lot going on in my head to get disorganized, I guess. It's mostly just kind of quiet. So, no, I haven't noticed having a hard time following a train of thought.\n\nDoctor: When you are around other people, do you feel uncomfortable or anxious, or do you just feel indifferent toward them?\n\nPatient: I guess I just feel... indifferent. I don't really feel anxious or scared or anything like that. It's more like I just don't care. I don't feel any connection or reason to be around them, so I just... don't want to be. I don't feel that way.\n\nDoctor: About how long has it been since you started feeling this way and stopped doing your usual activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... this whole decline, all of this happening... it's been going on since I started the community-college program. Even when my mom asks what's wrong, I... My thoughts don't really get jumbled up like that. I mean, when I'm just sitting there, I'm not really... I don't have a lot going on in my head to get disorganized, I guess. It's mostly just kind of quiet. So, no, I haven't noticed having a hard time following a train of thought.\n\nDoctor: When you are around other people, do you feel uncomfortable or anxious, or do you just feel indifferent toward them?\n\nPatient: I guess I just feel... indifferent. I don't really feel anxious or scared or anything like that. It's more like I just don't care. I don't feel any connection or reason to be around them, so I just... don't want to be. It's just easier to be by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have any unusual beliefs or experiences, such as feeling like you have a special connection to certain events or that you can sense things others cannot?\n\nPatient: Well, I do have this one thing.
